# Fable 3 (Fable3.exe) â€” decompilation

Functional reconstruction of `Fable3.exe`, built to behave the same as the
original. Where a function can be made **byte-identical** to the original
without padding, it is (labeled `[BYTE-IDENTICAL]` in the source and tracked in
the chaos-viewer atlas); otherwise it carries a `[NOT byte-identical]` note
explaining why (see **Byte-identity status** below). Organized into **module
folders** so it can be filled in one subsystem at a time.

- **Toolchain:** VS2008 SP1 (cl 15.00.30729 / link 9.00.30729), Win SDK 7.1,
  flags `/MT /GL /O2 /Ob2`, image base `0x400000`, GUI subsystem.
- **Build:** `build_vs2008.bat` â†’ `Release/Win32/Fable3.exe` (release; the
  debug variant `build_vs2008_debug.bat` adds `/DEBUG /Zi` +
  `FABLE3_WAIT_FOR_DEBUGGER=1` and outputs the same exe).
- **Ground truth:** IDA database `../Fable3.exe.i64` (symbols stripped). Every
  function keeps its IDA address-range annotation
  (`// name  (0x<start> - 0x<end>)`) so it maps back to the original binary.

## Layout

```
main.cpp                    application entry point (WinMain)
build_vs2008.bat            compiles + links every module .cpp (release)
build_vs2008_debug.bat      same, with /DEBUG /Zi + FABLE3_WAIT_FOR_DEBUGGER=1

framework/                  refcounted framework init / shutdown
  framework.h
  framework.cpp             FrameworkInit  (0x1A02B50)
                            FrameworkShutdown (0x1A02E70)
                            ComputeExeDirectory (0x1A02870) + ExeDirectory()
                            ReleaseGlobalObject (0x4017B0) â€” release the shared debug-handler pair

common/                     engine-wide memory / buffer helpers
  common.h
  common.cpp                LHAlloc (0x1A035C0, heap alloc),
                            LHFree (0x1A032C0, heap free),
                            LHAllocAligned (0x1A03630) — the heap's _aligned_malloc
                            (LHAlloc(size+align+8), align the block up, 8-byte
                            front header {offset, align}); the allocator Havok's
                            global memory interface is wired to,
                            JmpLHAllocAligned (0x619990) — 5-byte `jmp LHAllocAligned`
                            thunk RunFrontEnd stores in Havok's aligned-allocate slot
                            (off_1D8C850), LHFreeAligned (0x1A034D0) — the heap's
                            _aligned_free (recovers the raw block via [result-8] and
                            frees it through the heap table), JmpLHFreeAligned
                            (0x6199A0) — 5-byte `jmp LHFreeAligned` thunk RunFrontEnd
                            stores in the aligned-free slot (off_1D8C854),
                            SharedBufferAssign (0x403FE0, buffer assign),
                            SharedBufferAssignWide (0x404CC0) â€” resize a block into a
                            wide-text buffer (space-filled, wide terminator),
                            SharedBufferFree (0x403820, buffer free),
                            SharedBufferCreate (0x1A01360) â€” allocate + fill a counted buffer from (src, size)
                            InitSharedSlot (0x1A00C30) â€” shared-slot ctor (+ g_Addend counter)
                            ReleaseSharedSlot (0x1A01320) â€” shared-slot dtor (refcount + free)
                            ReleaseSharedSlotWide (0x19FFA70) â€” wide shared-slot dtor
                            (refcount + free, drops the wide counter g_WideSlotCount 0x2075C4C)
                            CopySharedSlotWide (0x19FFA20) â€” wide shared-slot copy ctor
                            (null + register in g_WideSlotCount, share src block; thiscall, retn 4)
                            SetSharedSlot (0x1A01CC0) â€” general shared-slot setter (thiscall, retn 8)
                            GetLocaleEntry (0x6195C0) â€” per-LCID localized-string table entry lookup

                            GetSharedSlotWide (0x19FF560) â€” wide shared-slot string getter
                            F3SharedBuffer struct

                            (0x61E930) / BankTreeIterNext (0x61EC30) / BankTreeRotateLeft
```

## Conventions

- One folder per subsystem, each with a `*.h` (public declarations) and a
  `*.cpp` (implementation). `main.cpp` only holds `WinMain` and includes the
  module headers.
- Public functions are declared in the module header and called by name;
  module-private helpers are `static` in the `.cpp`.
- Functions are named after their IDA symbol once identified (e.g.
  `sub_1A02B50` â†’ `FrameworkInit`); unknown ones keep the `sub_XXXXXX` name.
- Stubs carry the IDA decompile (or a summary) in a comment block marked
  `[STUB]` so the real body can be slotted in later.
- To add a module: create `folder/folder.h` + `folder/folder.cpp`, then add
  `..\..\folder\folder.cpp` to `SRCS` and the `.obj` to the `link` line in
  both `build_vs2008.bat` and `build_vs2008_debug.bat`.

## Byte-identity status

A function byte-matches the original only if it contains **no** relative
`E8` call, `E9` jmp, or `0F 8x` long conditional jump â€” those depend on the
link-time layout. Short jumps and absolute-address memory ops are placement-
independent. The current state of the implemented functions:

| Function | Bytes | Byte-identical? | Why |
|----------|------|-----------------|-----|
| `CLegacyMemoryFileReleaseFileHandle` (0x17FDC60, 0x17FDC60 - 0x17FDC7B) | 27 | **yes** | [BYTE-IDENTICAL] no calls / no absolute refs / no high-VA globals -- the body is the original's exact instruction sequence (push esi / mov esi,ecx / mov eax,[esi+8] / test/jz / mov ecx,[eax] / mov edx,[ecx+8] / push eax / call edx / [skip:] mov dword ptr [esi+8],0 / pop esi / retn), emitted as naked asm so it matches the original 27 bytes exactly. The LHSFS::CLegacyMemoryFile's vtable slot 2 (release the object's +8 file handle through that handle's own vtable slot 2, stdcall, the handle object as the argument), null +8, and return the release result. IDA renamed sub_17FDC60 -> CLegacyMemoryFileReleaseFileHandle. |
| `CLegacyMemoryFileReadChunk` (0x17FDC80, 0x17FDC80 - 0x17FDCC8) | 72 | **yes** | [BYTE-IDENTICAL] no E8 calls / no E9 jumps / no 0F 8x long conditionals / no high-VA globals -- the body is the original's exact instruction sequence (sub esp,8 / push esi / push edi / mov edi,[esp+0x18] / mov esi,ecx / test/jle / mov ecx,[esi+8] / test/jz / mov eax,[esp+0x14] / push ebx / mov [esp+0xc],eax / mov eax,[esi+0xc] / push 0 / cdq / push edi / push edx / mov [esp+0x1c],0 / mov ebx,[ecx] / push eax / mov eax,[ebx+0xc] / lea edx,[esp+0x1c] / push edx / call eax / add [esi+0xc],edi / pop ebx / pop edi / pop esi / add esp,8 / retn 0Ch), emitted as naked asm so it matches the original 72 bytes exactly. The LHSFS::CLegacyMemoryFile's vtable slot 3 (an IFile read/seek-style method): when the caller's count argument (the first stack arg, a3) is > 0 and the object's +8 file handle is non-null, stage an {out-pointer, 0} buffer, dispatch the handle's own vtable slot 3 (offset 0x0C, thiscall, the handle object as the argument) with (out=&buffer, extra=this+0x0C, extra>>31, count, 0), then bump this+0x0C by the count (thiscall, obj in ECX, 3 stack args, callee-cleanup retn 0Ch, result in EAX; the return is the vt[3] call's result, on the bail path whatever the caller left in EAX). IDA renamed sub_17FDC80 -> CLegacyMemoryFileReadChunk. |
| `CLegacyMemoryFileWrite` (0x17FDCD0, 0x17FDCD0 - 0x17FDD12) | 66 | **yes** | [BYTE-IDENTICAL] no E8 calls / no E9 jumps / no 0F 8x long conditionals / no high-VA globals -- the body is the original's exact instruction sequence (sub esp,8 / push esi / mov esi,ecx / mov ecx,[esi+8] / test,ecx / jz short / mov eax,[esp+0x10] / push ebx / push edi / mov edi,[esp+0x1c] / mov [esp+0xc],eax / mov eax,[esi+0xc] / cdq / push edi / push edx / mov [esp+0x18],0 / mov ebx,[ecx] / push eax / mov eax,[ebx+0x18] / lea edx,[esp+0x18] / push edx / call eax / add [esi+0xc],edi / pop edi / pop ebx / pop esi / add esp,8 / retn 0Ch), emitted as naked asm so it matches the original 66 bytes exactly. The LHSFS::CLegacyMemoryFile's vtable slot 4 (the handle's own vtable slot 6, an IFile write-style method): when the object's +8 file handle is non-null, stage an {a2, 0} buffer, dispatch the handle's own vtable slot 6 (offset 0x18, thiscall, the handle object as the argument) with (out=&buffer, extra=this+0x0C, extra>>31, count=a3), then bump this+0x0C by a3 (thiscall, obj in ECX, 3 stack args, callee-cleanup retn 0Ch, result in EAX; the return is the vt[6] call's result, on the bail path whatever the caller left in EAX). IDA renamed sub_17FDCD0 -> CLegacyMemoryFileWrite. |
| `CLegacyMemoryFileSetCounter` (0x17FDD20, 0x17FDD20 - 0x17FDD30) | 16 | **yes** | [BYTE-IDENTICAL] no calls / no absolute refs / no high-VA globals -- the body is the original's exact instruction sequence (cmp [ecx+8],0 / jz short / mov eax,[esp+4] / mov [ecx+0Ch],eax / retn 4), emitted as naked asm so it matches the original 16 bytes exactly. The LHSFS::CLegacyMemoryFile's vtable slot 5: when the object's +8 file handle is non-null, store the caller's value (a2, the first stack arg) into the +0xC extra counter and return it (thiscall, obj in ECX, callee-cleanup retn 4, result in EAX; on the bail path EAX is whatever the caller left in it). The sibling counter-adder (sub_17FDD30 -> CLegacyMemoryFileAddCounter) and the sibling counter-getter (sub_17FDD40 -> CLegacyMemoryFileGetCounter) are now both byte-identical. IDA renamed sub_17FDD20 -> CLegacyMemoryFileSetCounter. |
| `CLegacyMemoryFileGetLength` (0x17FDD50, 0x17FDD50 - 0x17FDD5A) | 10 | **yes** | [BYTE-IDENTICAL] no E8 calls / no E9 jmps / no 0F 8x long conditionals / no absolute refs / no high-VA globals -- the body is the original's exact instruction sequence (mov ecx,[ecx+8] / mov eax,[ecx] / mov edx,[eax+1Ch] / jmp edx), emitted as naked asm so it matches the original 10 bytes exactly. The LHSFS::CLegacyMemoryFile's vtable slot 9: a tail-dispatch forwarder -- forwards to the object's +8 file handle (the inner LHVFS::CMemoryFile): tail-call the inner object's vtable slot 7 (offset 0x1C) with the inner object as `this` (thiscall: obj in ECX, obj = the inner object) and return that slot's result. The inner object's slot 7 (sub_18A8A40) reads the inner object's +0x20 field and returns it -- the in-memory file's length (set from the source file's size by `MemoryFileReadFromSource`, or 0 for an empty file). The tail jump forwards any stack args to the inner slot, so the ABI is the inner slot's thiscall + cdecl hybrid. IDA renamed sub_17FDD50 -> CLegacyMemoryFileGetLength. |
| `CLegacyMemoryFileSlot8Stub` (0x17FD6F0, 0x17FD6F0 - 0x17FD6F3) | 3 | **yes** | [BYTE-IDENTICAL] no calls / no absolute refs / no high-VA globals -- the body is the original's exact instruction sequence (retn 4), emitted as naked asm so it matches the original 3 bytes exactly. The LHSFS::CLegacyMemoryFile's vtable slot 8: a no-op IFile stub -- thiscall, obj in ECX, one stack arg, callee-cleanup retn 4, no result (EAX is whatever the caller left in it). IDA renamed sub_17FD6F0 -> CLegacyMemoryFileSlot8Stub. |
| `CLegacyMemoryFileAddCounter` (0x17FDD30, 0x17FDD30 - 0x17FDD40) | 16 | **yes** | [BYTE-IDENTICAL] no calls / no absolute refs / no high-VA globals -- the body is the original's exact instruction sequence (cmp [ecx+8],0 / jz short / mov eax,[esp+4] / add [ecx+0Ch],eax / retn 4), emitted as naked asm so it matches the original 16 bytes exactly. The LHSFS::CLegacyMemoryFile's vtable slot 6: when the object's +8 file handle is non-null, add the caller's value (a2, the first stack arg) into the +0xC extra counter and return the added value (thiscall, obj in ECX, callee-cleanup retn 4, result in EAX; on the bail path EAX is whatever the caller left in it). IDA renamed sub_17FDD30 -> CLegacyMemoryFileAddCounter. |
| `CLegacyMemoryFileGetCounter` (0x17FDD40, 0x17FDD40 - 0x17FDD4D) | 13 | **yes** | [BYTE-IDENTICAL] no calls / no absolute refs / no high-VA globals -- the body is the original's exact instruction sequence (cmp [ecx+8],0 / jz short / mov eax,[ecx+0Ch] / retn / xor eax,eax / retn), emitted as naked asm so it matches the original 13 bytes exactly. The LHSFS::CLegacyMemoryFile's vtable slot 7: when the object's +8 file handle is non-null, return the +0xC extra counter; otherwise return 0 (thiscall, obj in ECX, no stack args, plain retn, result in EAX). IDA renamed sub_17FDD40 -> CLegacyMemoryFileGetCounter. |
| `XmlWriteBool` (0x17DB3E0, 0x17DB3E0 - 0x17DB409) | 25 | **yes** | [BYTE-IDENTICAL] no E8 call / no E9 jmp / no 0F 8x long conditional / no high-VA globals -- the body is the original's exact instruction sequence (mov eax,[esp+4] / mov dl,[eax] / mov eax,[ecx] / mov eax,[eax+48h] / mov byte ptr [esp+4],dl / lea edx,[esp+4] / push edx / call eax / retn 4), emitted as naked asm so it matches the original 25 bytes exactly. The write-stream vtable[5] slot (the write-side value-writer for bools): thiscall, obj in ECX, a BYTE out-pointer pushed by the caller, callee-cleanup retn 4, result in EAX -- the bool byte is loaded from the out-param, the out-param slot is overwritten with the byte (the callee, vtable[18] = sub_17DB5E0, reads the byte from that slot), the slot address is pushed and the serializer is called and its result returned. This build does not install vtable[18] (the 163-byte LHXML value serializer and its callees -- InternXmlString / the value-type table / the attribute setter -- are a remaining stub), so no code path in this build invokes this slot; the install keeps the vtable shape. IDA renamed sub_17DB3E0 -> XmlWriteBool. |
| `XmlWriteInt2` (0x17DB400, 0x17DB400 - 0x17DB45D) | 93 | no | [NOT byte-identical] placement-dependent (relative E8 calls to XmlScratchLockEnter / XmlScratchEnsure / XmlScratchGet / __snprintf_s / sub_17E7440 (the value-type table lookup) / InternXmlString / XmlScratchLockLeave). The write-stream vtable[23] slot in the original (the write-side value-writer for signed ints; the project installs it at vt[8] in this build's model; the sibling of the uint writer sub_17DB460 -- '%u' with a3=2 -- and the float writer sub_17DB4C0 -- '%f %X' with a3=3): thiscall, obj in ECX, the out-param (the int to write) on the stack, callee-cleanup retn 4. The int value is loaded from the out-param, the scratch buffer is ensured to 16 bytes, the int is formatted as "%d" into it, the "int" value-type name (g_xmlValueTypes[1], the 0x10-byte ensure / format / interning chain) is interned (InternXmlString -- a remaining stub in this build, returns 0), the conditional dispatch through the stream's vtable[11] (the "type" attribute setter -- byte 20 = sectionFound != 0, thiscall, obj in ECX, the "type" string and the value buffer pushed by the caller, callee-cleanup retn 8) is skipped because this build installs only vt[1]/[3]/[5]/[8]/[9] (the slot is null), and the section-info slot (+0x10) is refreshed from the current section (+0x0C) before the scratch lock is left. IDA renamed sub_17DB400 -> XmlWriteInt2. |
| `XmlWriteInt` (0x17DB340, 0x17DB340 - 0x17DB359) | 25 | no | [NOT byte-identical] placement-dependent (relative E8 call) + the flattened-vtable model: the original's 25-byte body reads the out-param's int into a stack slot (`mov eax,[esp+4]; mov edx,[eax]; mov [esp+4],edx; lea edx,[esp+4]; push edx`) and forwards it to the write-side signed-int value-writer (vtable[23], XmlWriteInt2) -- this build installs the int writer at vtable[8] (offset 0x20), so the dispatch is re-targeted there (the original's vtable[23] does not exist in this build's 32-slot vtable). The write-stream vtable[9] slot in the original (the write-side int-writer wrapper; the sibling of the vt[8] uint wrapper sub_17DB360 -- the same shape with vtable[22]/sub_17DB460 '%u' behind it): thiscall, obj in ECX, the out-param on the stack, callee-cleanup retn 4, result in EAX -- the int value is loaded from the out-param into the stack slot (clobbering the argument slot with the value), the slot address is pushed, and the installed int value-writer is called with it (the call returns the writer's result). The int value-writer only reads *out (formats / interns / refreshes the section-info slot -- it never writes through the pointer), so the slot the original passes is effect-equivalent to the caller's out-param; the out-param itself is never written. Wired into `g_CXMLWriteStreamVtable[9]` by the write-stream ctor; no code path in this build dispatches it (the tree is built and serialized, but the write-side serializers still produce no stream output). | `BankTreeEraseRange` (0x624560) + the bank-tree cluster (`BankTreeLeftmost` 0x61E950 / `BankTreeRightmost` 0x61E930 / `BankTreeIterNext` 0x61EC30 / `BankTreeRotateLeft` 0x620910 / `BankTreeRotateRight` 0x61E990 / `BankTreeFreeSubtree` 0x621630 / `BankTreeEraseNode` 0x623630) | 227 (target) | no | [NOT byte-identical] implemented in C++ (`engine/banktree.cpp`) against the bank container's MSVC `_Rb_tree`. The tree object (0x20 bytes, in ECX = the container's +0xCC member) is `{+0x00 head node, +0x18 sentinel node, +0x1C count}`; the 32-byte node is `{left +0, parent +4, right +8, key +0xC, object +0x10, color +0x14 (0 red / 1 black), sentinel-flag +0x15}` (nonzero sentinel flag = a head/sentinel node, not a data node). `BankTreeEraseRange` (thiscall + `retn 0x14` = 5 stack args, declared object-first) validates the two bound dwords against the head node (`__invalid_parameter_noinfo` -> `F3InvalidParam` when the bound is null or != the head), then either takes the **fast path** (start == the sentinel's leftmost && end == the sentinel: free the whole root subtree via `BankTreeFreeSubtree`, self-loop the sentinel, zero the count) or the **generic path** (advance the in-order iterator `start` and, for each node through `end`, splice + rebalance-erase it via `BankTreeEraseNode`, decrementing the count each time); it writes `{head, end}` into the 2-dword out and returns the out. The helpers are standard MSVC `_Rb_tree` primitives: `BankTreeLeftmost` / `BankTreeRightmost` follow left / right child pointers to the extremes; `BankTreeIterNext` advances a `{tag, node}` iterator to its in-order successor (right-subtree-leftmost, else walk up while the node is its parent's right child); `BankTreeRotateLeft` / `BankTreeRotateRight` are the standard color-preserving rotations (the root update goes through the sentinel's +4); `BankTreeFreeSubtree` recursively frees the right subtree, frees the node, then continues on the left child until the sentinel; `BankTreeEraseNode` is the full single-node erase + red-black rebalance (it throws `std::out_of_range` on a sentinel node, via `BankTreeThrowOutOfRange`, and uses the successor's right child / parent as the rebalance start in the two-children case). Replaces the no-op `sub_624560` stub in `gamemain.cpp`; `DestroyBankContainer` (0x626EF0) now calls it (the full-tree clear, the fast path). The cluster is only reached from the bank mount/destroy callers, so it is not exercised by the engine test. |

The helpers are the standard MSVC `_Rb_tree` primitives (color / sentinel flag at +0x2C / +0x2D): `NameMapLeftmost` / `NameMapRightmost` follow left / right child pointers to the extremes; `NameMapIterNext` advances a `{tag, node}` iterator to its in-order successor (right-subtree-leftmost, else walk up while the node is its parent's right child); `NameMapRotateLeft` / `NameMapRotateRight` are the standard color-preserving rotations (the root update goes through the sentinel's +4); `NameMapFreeSubtree` recursively frees the right subtree, destroys the node's in-tree wide-string payload (free the heap buffer when cap >= 8, then cap = 7, len = 0, buffer low word = 0), frees the node, then continues on the left child until the sentinel; `NameMapEraseNode` is the full single-node erase + red-black rebalance (it throws `std::out_of_range` on a sentinel node, via `NameMapThrowOutOfRange`, and destroys the payload before the `LHFree` of the node). Replaces the no-op `sub_5AB5F0` stub in `gamemain.cpp`; `DestroyBankContainer` (0x626EF0) now calls it (the full-tree clear, the fast path). The cluster is only reached from the bank destroy callers, so it is not exercised by the engine test. |

mode 0 read-write + OPEN_ALWAYS + 0x48000080, mode 2 read-write + 0x00800080, mode 5 read-write + CREATE_ALWAYS + 0x08000000 -- all share 3 and return the handle in both the read and write slots; any other mode fails immediately), on a handle == -1 failure the `Win32FileSystemReportOpenFailure` (the `GetLastError` three-bucket classifier -- bucket 2 for access-denied 2/3/5, bucket 0 for 0xF/0x12/0x15/0x20/0x26/0xA1, bucket 1 otherwise -- dispatches the file-system sub-object's (+0x28) vt[6] with (bucket, `GetWideStringHash`(path)) or falls back to `XApiErrorReport` and returns the bucket-2 flag) and the wide / F3WString scratch is reset in place; on success the 72-byte CWin32File is built by `Win32FileInit` (+0x08 read handle, +0x0C write handle [the original's +0x0C / +0x3C slots hold the caller's return address -- a codegen artifact of the single-word push at the one call site, so Write / Seek fail and Read always takes the synchronous path in the original binary; reproduced with a naked caller-return-address helper], +0x10 0x1000, +0x14 -0x1000, +0x1C path description (fs+0x24, AddRef'd), +0x20 file-system sub-object (fs+0x28, AddRef'd), +0x30..+0x37 position 0, +0x38 the relative-path F3WString pointer, g_Win32FileVtable 13 slots: [0] Win32FileQueryInterface (0x5B57A0) / [1] AddRef (InterlockedIncrement) / [2] Release (InterlockedDecrement-to-zero -> vt[11]) / [3-10] the 8 IFile methods (remaining stubs) / [11] the deleting dtor (the dtor body then `LHFree` through the heap table) / [12] RTTI stub). `Win32FileDtor` closes +0x08 unconditionally + +0x0C when distinct and != -1, runs the verbose `CLOSE: #%08x #%08x` log through the `dword_20B5FA8` / `dword_20B5FB0` verbose-log globals (both image-zero in this build, so the log path stays dead), releases the +0x20 sub-object then the +0x1C path description through their vt[2], and the deleting dtor `LHFree`s on the free flag. `Win32FileSystemGetPath` (vt[8]) copies the wrapper's embedded SSO root path into a provided 36-byte F3WString (the cap < 8 SSO / heap-pointer split, cap 7 / len 0 / low word 0 before the assign, no normalisation -- the original's non-normalising sibling of the path builder). The vt[4] dispatch convention in the open-setup is thiscall (fsObj in ECX, 3 stack args, as the original calls it at 0x17FE39B) -- the project dispatch is a thiscall cast to match. Replaces the `Win32FileSystemOpenStub` no-op wired into g_Win32FileSystemVtable[4] (which reported no source, leaving the video file unopened) and the 0x5AF5E0 / 0x18AB350 stubs; the vtable grows from 8 to 11 slots (vt[8] = the root-path copy, vt[10] = the deleting dtor, the 11-slot original layout). |
| `Win32FileQueryInterface` (0x5B57A0) | 108 | no | [NOT byte-identical] placement-dependent (the relative E8 call to the 16-byte IID compare sub_5AE060 + the indirect AddRef dispatch through the object's vtable + the high-VA IID globals). The shared vt[0] QueryInterface body for the LHVFS IFile-derived classes (CWin32File / CMemoryFile / CBasicFileWindow / CCompositeFile / CBankFile / CCompressedBankFile / CXContentFile / CAddRelQueryImp -- all 8 classes store the same vt[0] body in their vtables). stdcall, 3 stack args (self / riid / ppv), retn 0x0C. If riid matches either accepted IID (IUnknown 0x1BF7604 / the IFile IID 0x1BF7874), *ppv is set to the object, the object's vtable[1] (AddRef) is dispatched, and S_OK (0) is returned. Otherwise *ppv is cleared and 0x80004002 (E_NOINTERFACE) is returned. The original has no ppv-null check. Wired into g_Win32FileVtable[0] (replacing the 0 stub); the other 7 classes share the same body in their vtables but have no vtable in this build yet. |

| `Win32FileSystemHashResolve` (0x18AACA0) | 47 | no | [NOT byte-identical] placement-dependent (the relative E8 calls to the manifest-index helper (sub_5AF880, reimplemented here) and the vt[4] dispatch). The +0x2C 32-byte CFileManifest wraps a MSVC debug-iterator-like dynamic array -- the +0x08 slot (manifest[2]) is a self-tag block whose first DWORD is the metadata pointer (the [lower, upper) bounds at +0x0C / +0x10), manifest[5] (+0x14) is the current position, manifest[6] (+0x18) is the end. The index is checked against (manifest[6] - manifest[5]) / 36 (sub_5AF880's magic-constant signed division, throwing std::out_of_range on a violation); the current position advances by 36 bytes per index step and is re-checked against the [lower, upper] bounds (v4 = the first DWORD of the self-tag block, the metadata pointer); __invalid_parameter_noinfo paths reproduce as F3InvalidParam (no-op, return gracefully -- the manifest is never null or out-of-range in this build). The vt[4] method is dispatched on the wrapper with the out slot / advanced position / mode (the same arguments the original passes); the out slot is returned. The 8-byte {value, SEH block} wrapper ctor (RunFrontEnd 0x61D6C4: `LHAlloc(8)` + this ctor, stored at engine member 0xB4, released by `CGameEngineDestroy` via the SEH block at +4): zeroes both words, `LHAlloc(96)`, constructs the 96-byte game-impl (the 1-word / 4-byte flag pattern + the 4-byte + 28/32-byte self-referencing sub-lists at impl+0x10 / impl+0x30 — a 4-byte block holding the impl pointer + a 28-/32-byte block whose words 0/1/2 are all the block itself with flag bytes at +0x18/+0x19 / +0x1C/+0x1D — + the 20-byte float block {1.5707964f, 1.5707964f, 0.5f, 0.5f, 0.5f} at impl+0x50 with its 12-byte SEH block { refcount=1, handler=JmpLHFree2, arg=the float block }) and then `RegisterWrapperSeh` stores the impl + a fresh 12-byte SEH block { refcount=1, handler=ReleaseGameImpl, arg=impl } at the wrapper's +4, returning the wrapper. The impl ctor also writes the high-VA global `dword_1DC269C` (the live game-impl pointer). `ReleaseGameImpl` (the wrapper's SEH handler) tears down the impl — `GameImplTeardown`: clears `dword_1DC269C`, releases the +0x5C / +0x54 SEH blocks (handler + `LHFree` on zero), clears the +0x58/+0x5C / +0x50/+0x54 / +0x48/+0x4C / +0x28/+0x2C tail slots, frees the 4-byte / 28-byte / 32-byte blocks — and `LHFree`s the impl; then the teardown's 12-byte wrapper block is freed and the wrapper itself. The two `ResetSubList*` callees are no-ops for the constructed flag-set blocks (their flag bytes are always 1, so the recursive release finalizer — `ReleaseListNode28` / `ReleaseListNode32` — dispatches the immediate no-op fast path; the writes are redundant self-refs plus the flag-clear word). The IDA names `sub_712430` / `sub_712120` / `sub_7158A0` / `sub_715920` / `sub_716670` / `sub_716760` / `sub_7136D0` / `sub_7172D0` / `sub_7172B0` / `sub_711DD0` / `j_LHFree_2` / `sub_715810` / `sub_714C40` were renamed accordingly. |

| `LHAllocAligned` (0x1A03630) | 46 | no | [NOT byte-identical] placement-dependent (relative call to `LHAlloc`). The aligned counterpart of `LHAlloc` (the heap's `_aligned_malloc`): `LHAlloc(size + align + 8)`, round the raw block up to the next multiple of `align` (power of two), and write the 8-byte front header `[result-8] = result - raw` (the front offset into the raw block) + `[result-4] = align`; returns the aligned pointer. The counterpart free `LHFreeAligned` (0x1A034D0) recovers the raw pointer via `[result-8]` and frees it through the heap table's free slot. This is the allocator Havok's global memory interface is wired to: `RunFrontEnd` (0x61D40E) installs `JmpLHAllocAligned` into `off_1D8C850` (the aligned-allocate slot; the free slot `off_1D8C854` gets the sibling thunk `JmpLHFreeAligned` -> `LHFreeAligned`), and the Havok `hkFreeListMemoryServer` / `hkDefaultFreeListMemoryServer` objects call through that slot pair (e.g. `sub_186E690`: `off_1D8C850(16, 16)`). cdecl (size, align on the stack). |

The 5-byte thunk `RunFrontEnd` stores in Havok's global aligned-allocate slot `off_1D8C850` (a sibling of `JmpLHFree` in the 0x619xxx engine-thunk cluster). |

All three graphics-entity callees are now implemented (BuildGraphicsEntityPool + RegisterGraphicsEntities, plus the SyncGraphicsEntities (sub_18D02E0) sync commit), so the pool is zeroed, rebuilt, and the 224 descriptors are registered into the graphics-entity object pool (0x211A408) with the newly-registered ones appended to the dirty list. Rebuilds the frontend graphics-entity pool contents and the 266-slot entity index list: 29 pre-loop 24-byte pool records ({+0x0E=1, +0x14=id}, one index entry each), four 0x70-byte records with flag bytes +0x0E/+0x2A/+0x46 = 1 and t0/t1/t2 from the rdata table at 0x1D9DF98 (four index entries each), and twenty 0x1C-byte records each associated with eleven 0x230-byte sub-structures (eleven index entries each); the 739 non-zero pool field writes + 265 index entries are applied from embedded tables (the caller memsets the pool to zero first, so only the non-zero writes are reproduced) and the counter is set to 265. Sole caller: `ResetGraphicsEntities` (0x18D1960); replaces the sub_18CFA70 no-op stub. |
| `RegisterGraphicsEntities` (0x18D04D0) | 5263 | no | [NOT byte-identical] a 5263-byte loop-heavy data-registration routine that refs the high-VA graphics-entity object pool `0x211A408` (8200-byte objects at 0x2008 stride), the frontend entity descriptor pool `0x2120420`, the dirty-registration list `dword_2073940` (count `dword_2073938`), and the rdata source values `0x1D9DF8C`..`0x1D9E0CC` at their original addresses; no function calls (pure data registration). Re-registers the 224 frontend graphics-entity descriptors (built by BuildGraphicsEntityPool) into the per-object lists of the graphics-entity object pool and appends each newly-registered descriptor to the dirty list. Each 28-byte descriptor record `{+0x00 ptr, +0x04 key, +0x08 mask, +0x0E idx, +0x18 flag}` is processed when its key != the expected source value: if `(object.maskSrc & mask) == 0` the `{ptr, key}` entry is appended to the object's list (object.maskSrc is not modified, only the descriptor's mask field accumulates it), the key is set to expected, and if the flag was clear the ptr is pushed onto the dirty list and the flag set. The 224 descriptors are laid out as a straight-line run of 20, a 4x4 do-while over 16 (expected key 0), a 20x9 second loop over the descriptor tables (iteration offset 28*i for i<16 and 28*i+0x1A5C for i>=16), and a final 8-descriptor tail; the expected-key source values and the IEEE-754 float constants (0x42800000 / 0x3F800000) are embedded. At runtime only objects 0..2 of the pool are referenced (max 112 entries / object, well under the 1024-entry list), so the {ptr,key} list never overflows. The object pool, the dirty list, and the high-offset descriptor tail (past the 0x1DF8 build pool) are modelled as zero-initialized statics (they sit in BSS, which the Windows loader zeros). Callers: `ResetGraphicsEntities` (0x18D1960) and sub_57AF70 (the return value â€” the last descriptor's object base â€” is ignored by both); replaces the sub_18D04D0 no-op stub. |

Walks `unk_1BD3020` (stopping at the 0 sentinel); per D3DFORMAT, queries the live D3D9 object for the mode count (vtable[6]) and, per mode, the 16-byte descriptor (vtable[7], {width, height, refresh, pixelFormat}); a mode with width < height is skipped when (dm->f28 & 0x20) is set. The descriptor's pixel format selects the texture source-descriptor table row (row[0] key; the index resets to row 0 when no row matches). An exact match -- width, height AND refresh all equal the request's and the row's bits-per-element ratio (row[2] / row[1] / row[1], signed integer division) equal dm->f12 -- is stored into *out16 and returned immediately, bypassing the rest of the loop; the remaining modes are scored (float, fabs of each signed 32-bit difference) `score = (|w-dmW|+|h-dmH|)/(dmW+dmH)*10 + |ratio-f12|/f12*10 + |f24-refresh|/f24`, keeping the strictly-smallest (initial best 1e30f, `fcomip` + `jbe`); when no mode scores better the all-zero descriptor is returned (width 0). A null-guard on the D3D9 object is added where the original would fault (a pre-device slot). Renamed in IDA from `sub_18F1510`; sole caller `TrySetDisplayMode`. |

The post-grow rebind of the heap's alloc-node chain (the heap+0x2C sub-object's +0x2C head; a node is {flags, table, next}). For each node, every global of the node's table is enumerated through the new cluster: a VM table is {+0x04 hash count-1, +0x08 20-byte hash rows {key, type, value, size, next}, +0x0C 8-byte dense rows {size, state}, +0x10 dense count}; the enumerator (`KoreNextGlobal`, thiscall + `retn 12`: the in/out 2-word key cursor {index-as-float, 3} while dense or {row key, row type} while hash, the out 2-word entry {value, size}, the vm-core arg dead) walks the dense rows first (`KoreNextDenseGlobal`: skip state-0 rows, the key resumes at the whole-number float in it, on a hit the key becomes {index+1 as float (2^32 bias when negative), 3}; exhausted â†’ `KoreNextHashGlobal` from row 0) then the hash rows (`KoreNextHashGlobal`: first occupied row at/after the start index, count = +0x04 word + 1; `KoreNextGlobalByKey` finds the key's row â€” `KoreFindGlobalRow`: empty table / type-0 key â†’ -1, integer key â†’ `KoreFindFreeBlock` (its -4 miss passes through), string key â†’ `KoreFindGlobalStringRow` (type-4 chain walk), otherwise the probe from `KoreGlobalRowHash` (type 3: (key>>2) * 0x9E3779B1 & mask; type 4: (key ^ ((key^(key>>3))>>5)) & mask; else the x87-truncated int & mask) + the chain walk (row type == key type and, for type 3, exact float key match (unordered NaN falls to the chain) or dword-equal key) â€” and advances past it). Each enumerated entry is checked twice: the key half ({row key, row type}) against node flag bit 2 and the value half ({row value, row size}) against node flag bit 1; when the half's type is a reference type (5..10 or 12) and its payload header byte is unflagged (low bit clear at pointer-4 for type 4 / 7, else pointer-8), the global is reset through `KoreTableSet` (now implemented, see its row below) to the "not found" marker pair, `KoreDebugMarkDirty` (now implemented, see its row below) is called when `*(*(vm-core)+0x04) + 0x3C == 1`, and the alloc budget (heap+0x2C sub-object +0x6C) is charged 5 per rebind; every third node additionally charges 1. | `KoreStructFieldGet` (sub_610BA0) + the value-key gettable pair (`KoreGlobalGetValue` 0x5DDB40 / `KoreGlobalGetByFloat` 0x5D3280) | 182 + 143 + 238 | no | [NOT byte-identical] all three are thiscall + retn-N hybrids (the instance / table in ECX) with relative E8 calls whose displacements change with the build's layout (`KoreStructFieldGet` -> `KoreGlobalGetValue`; `KoreGlobalGetValue` -> `KoreLookupStringTable` / `KoreGlobalGetByFloat` / `KoreFindGlobalRow`; `KoreGlobalGetByFloat` -> `__ftol2_sse`) and they ref the zero-initialized nil pair globals (dword_1DBCFE0 / dword_1DBCFE4) at high VA. `KoreStructFieldGet` (the struct arm of the VM's gettable-by-value-key opcode 0x0C, the getter counterpart of `KoreStructSetField` 0x612630): a string key (type 4) linearly scans the instance's prototype / descriptor table (the +0x04 pointer: the +0x00 field count, the 16-byte rows from +0x18 keyed by their +0x00 interned id); on a match the row's +13 descriptor byte selects the field slot, the value is the instance's +4*slot word and the type the packed byte at +4*(slot & 0xFC) + (slot & 3) (the same layout `KoreStructSetField` writes); a non-string key or scan miss falls back to the instance's +0x0C backing hash table through `KoreGlobalGetValue` (a null backing table yields the nil pair). `KoreGlobalGetValue` (4 args: table / out / vm-core hint / key slot): string keys (type 4) -> `KoreLookupStringTable`, number keys (type 3) -> `KoreGlobalGetByFloat`, anything else -> `KoreFindGlobalRow` (the vm-core hint is dead there) reading the {value, type} pair at +0x08 / +0x0C of the 20-byte row from the hash rows at the table's +0x08 (the -1 miss -> the nil pair). `KoreGlobalGetByFloat`: integer-valued keys (the `__ftol2_sse` truncation round-trips exactly) read dense row n - 1 of the 8-byte row array at the table's +0x0C (count at +0x10); otherwise probe the 20-byte rows at +0x08 at bucket (fistp the key toward +infinity, saturating) & the +0x04 mask and walk the next-pointer chain until a type-3 row whose key matches. The original's x87 flag tests are compiled against the ambient FPU status word and its row-key comparison duplicates the key instead of the row key, so these implement the intended semantics (round-trip / key equality, NaN never hits). The 0x0C interpreter case now dispatches type 5 -> `KoreGlobalGetValue`, type 12 -> `KoreStructFieldGet`, other -> the __index path (a nil result falls through to the __index path in every case, as in the original). `KoreMakeStructInstance` (sub_5E2970) was fixed to the original layout (word 1 = the prototype, word 3 = the backing table, size 4*BYTE1(proto[4*count+5])+4, the vm-core slot writes; the no-backing-table fast path sub_5D2D70 inlined) so the instance layout the getter reads matches. |
| `KoreGettableByName` (0x5E4420) + the __index resolver stub (`KoreGettableResolve` sub_5E30C0) | 212 (target) | no | [NOT byte-identical] placement-dependent (the relative E8 calls to `KoreLookupStringTable` / `KoreTablePanic` / `KoreLookupInternedString` / `KoreGettableResolve`) and refs the two high-VA panic-format strings (0x01AE15D4 "attempt to index a nil value" / 0x01AE15F4 "error in gettable_event"). The 6-arg cdecl gettable name resolver â€” the generic arm of the VM's gettable family, the 6-param sibling of `KoreTableGetGeneric` (0x5E45E0): same type-based name resolution, but it delegates to the recursive __index resolver `KoreGettableResolve` (sub_5E30C0) rather than `KoreGettableString`. Resolves the metatable lookup "name" for a value of a given type: types 5/7/12 read the name from the value's own fields (+0x00 / +0x04 / +0x08); any other type looks the type's name up in the global string table (base = *vmcore, key at base + 8*type + 372, the string table at base + 204, hash 4) and ORs in the low bit of the name when the resolved type word is not 5. Panics "attempt to index a nil value" when the name is 0; interns it (`KoreLookupInternedString(vmcore, name, 1)` -> the {value, type} qword; panics "error in gettable_event" when the type word is 0); and returns `KoreGettableResolve(vmcore, value, type, key, code, nameValue, nameType, depth)`. `KoreGettableResolve` (the recursive __index / gettable metamethod resolver, 0x5E30C0 - 0x5E3290: switches on the interned name's type word â€” 10/9 invoke the metamethod, 5 closure + recurse, 12 struct + recurse, else tail-call the name resolver â€” and panics "loop in gettable" at depth 0) is a remaining out-of-scope stub returning the zero nil pair. The target's former IDA name `KoreGlobalGetByFloat3` was renamed to `KoreGettableByName` (its behaviour is gettable name resolution, not a float-3 read); the 3-param `KoreGlobalGetByFloat3` stub used by the iterate-globals opcode (case 0xE) is a different function, now implemented as `KoreGlobalGetByInt` / `KoreGlobalGetByIntIndirect` (row below). |
| `KoreGlobalGetByInt` (sub_610B50) + `KoreGlobalGetByIntIndirect` (sub_610C60) | 78 + 64 | no | [NOT byte-identical] placement-dependent (relative E8 calls: `KoreGlobalGetByInt` -> `KoreGlobalGetValue`; `KoreGlobalGetByIntIndirect` -> `KoreGlobalGetByInt`) and they ref the zero-initialized nil-pair globals (dword_1DBCFE0 / dword_1DBCFE4) at high VA. The VM's gettable-by-integer-index helper pair â€” the real callees of the 0xE "iterate globals" opcode (the `KoreIterGlobals` C closure, sub_5E4CB0) â€” shared by ~10 other VM-internal sites (the value-stack / upvalue machinery and the module-registration helpers, through the get-by-int pair fetch `KoreGlobalGetByIntPair` at 0x5DF650). `KoreGlobalGetByInt` (0x610B50, cdecl 3 args: hint, tableObj, idx): the (idx - 1)-th 8-byte dense row of the row array at tableObj + 0x0C when in range (count at tableObj + 0x10), else the general value lookup `KoreGlobalGetValue(tableObj, out, hint, { (float)idx, 3 })` with a number key â€” returning the packed { value, type } pair (value low, type high). `KoreGlobalGetByIntIndirect` (0x610C60, thiscall + `retn 12`: this, out, hint, idx): reads the object's +0x0C field as the inner row base; when nonzero delegates to `KoreGlobalGetByInt(hint, this+0x0C, idx)`, else writes the nil pair; stores { value, type } through *out and returns *out. The 0xE case now checks the closure's function pointer by double-deref (`*(*(v7+0)) == KoreIterGlobals`), branches on the closure's +0x12 word (5 -> direct `KoreGlobalGetByInt` over the closure's +0x08 table object; else `KoreGlobalGetByIntIndirect`), stores the { value, type } result at +0x20/+0x24, then on a non-nil high word advances the +0x10 float iterator by 1.0 (+0x1C <- +0x14, +0x18 <- the new float) while a nil high word resets +0x14/+0x1C to 0 and +0x18 to the current float â€” replacing the earlier single-call `KoreGlobalGetByFloat3` placeholder (which returned a constant nil pair and was mislabeled sub_5E7FE0, which is actually `KoreGlobalSetFloat`). |
| `KoreIterGlobals` (sub_5E4CB0) | 110 | no | [NOT byte-identical] placement-dependent (relative E8 calls to `KoreGetIntegerArg` and `KoreGlobalGetByIntPair`). Replaces the `KoreIterGlobals` no-op stub â€” this is the real body of the built-in C closure the 0xE iterate-globals opcode special-cases by pointer (the closure's explicit Lua call path). Lua `next` over the globals table: argument 1 is the table object (or an object with a +0x0C row base), argument 2 the current integer index; it fetches entry (index+1) into a fresh top-of-stack cell (`KoreGlobalGetByIntPair` over the frame's first argument slot); a nil entry ends the iteration and returns 1 result (the nil cell), otherwise it stages float(index+1) in the next cell, copies the {float, 3} pair onto the index argument's slot, and returns 2 results (next index, the fetched value). Implementing it required the full builtin-argument-checker chain: `KoreGetIntegerArg` (sub_5E4970, 89 bytes: the Nth argument as an integer â€” zero from a number slot (including an absent argument) yields 0, zero from a non-number slot panics "integer expected, got %s" through `KoreBadArgumentPanic` (sub_5E39E0, 244 bytes: resolves the top frame; "bad argument #%d (%s)" when unresolvable, else "bad argument #%d to '%s' (%s)" with the function name and the "method" decrement, never returns â€” longjmp), the type name from `KoreArgTypeTag` (sub_408C50, 156 bytes: the slot's type word with closure tags 9/10 folded to 6, -1 when absent, indexed into `g_KoreTypeNames` as a signed offset so -1 hits the pre-array "no value" word), the panic message pushed as a string cell via `KoreVmVprintf` first); `KoreArgIsNumber` (sub_408140, 187 bytes, in-range slots via `KoreValueIsNumber` (sub_5D9420, 75 bytes: type 3 â†’ 1; type 4 â†’ 1 when `KoreFloatFromString` parses, the converted float clobbering the cell's value word in place)); `KoreArgNumberValue` (sub_4082C0, 197 bytes, 0.0 when absent). Shared index rules: positive = arguments from the frame base (vmcore+32, arg 1 at the base cell), negative = from the top (vmcore+28), special indices -10000 (the VM buffer's +0xCC cell), -10001 (the callee function slot's type word staged at vmcore+56/+60 as {type, 5} and aliased as a cell at vmcore+56), -10002 (vmcore+48), any other -1000x (the callee function object's payload fn - 8*idx - 80012). Two latent bugs found along the way were fixed: `KoreVmSprintf` now returns entry+8 (the interned payload bytes â€” `KoreBadArgumentPanic` uses it as the message C-string) instead of entry+2, and `KoreFloatFromString` (sub_5D6DE0, 155 bytes) is now the real VM strtod (payload at record+8, CRT strtod with endptr, a hex reparse through strtoul base 16 with +2^32 added back over the sign bit when strtod stops at x/X, trailing-whitespace skip, and 0 returned unless the whole payload was consumed â€” it previously always returned 1) and `KorePopNumberValue` (sub_5D93D0, 76 bytes) is the real number-cell reader (type 3 â†’ the stored float, type 4 â†’ the in-place parse, else 0.0 â€” previously the 0.0 stub). Unreachable in the engine test (the VM does not run Lua in this build). |
| `KoreStructFindSlot` (sub_606AE0) | 48 | **YES** | Reproduced the original's exact 48-byte sequence with `__declspec(naked)` inline asm (`engine/luavm.cpp`) â€” the body has no calls and no data references, so the bytes match at any placement. Original convention: thiscall + `retn 4` hybrid (the object in ECX, the key on the stack; declared object-first as `int(int obj, int key)`). The struct prototype / descriptor-table field-slot lookup that `KoreStructFieldGet`, the struct-assign guards, and the struct field get/set opcodes share: the object's +0x00 field count bounds a linear scan of the 16-byte rows from +0x18, each row keyed by its +0x00 interned field id; a match returns a pointer to the row, a zero count or a scan miss returns 0. Replaces the earlier `KoreIndexedGet` placeholder. |
| `KoreLookupOperatorName` (sub_5E3010) | 77 | no | [NOT byte-identical] placement-dependent (relative E8 calls to `KoreLookupMetamethod` and `KoreTablePanic`) and refs the high-VA data globals (the type-name array `off_1C830B8`, the operator-name table `off_1C83014`, and the zero-initialized nil pair `g_qword_1DBCFE0`). cdecl 4 args: `(vmcore, slot, op, opname)` where `slot` is a pointer to the 8-byte {value, type} operand pair on the value stack and `opname` is the operator's source string (e.g. `"#"` for `OP_LEN`). Resolves the operand's metamethod for the given operator via `KoreLookupMetamethod` (which returns a packed 64-bit {metamethodValue, metamethodType} pair in `eax:edx` â€” the zero-initialized nil pair `g_qword_1DBCFE0` when none is found); on a non-nil result (high word != 0) returns that pair, else panics via `KoreTablePanic(vmcore, "operator %s is not supported for %s %s", opname, opname, g_KoreTypeNames[*(int*)(slot+4)])` where the operand's type is the slot's high word (the slot+4 dword) indexing the real type-name table. The `OP_LEN` (opcode 0x31) call site in `KoreRunCode` was fixed to match the original: it stores the real `{metamethod, type}` pair the function returns into the temp frame (`t[0]` = value, `t[1]` = type, then the operand's own {value, type}) rather than the previously-hardcoded `{payload, 1}`, advances the value-stack frame pointer (`Size[7]`) by 16 bytes, then calls `KoreInvokeValue(vmcore, 1, 1, closure)`. `g_KoreTypeNames` was also corrected to the real `off_1C830B8` type-name data (`nil, boolean, userdata, number, string, table, function, userdata, thread, function, function, ui64, struct, 0, no value, nil`) since the previous table did not match the binary. Replaces the earlier no-op stub that returned a nil pair. |
| `KoreConcatSlots` (sub_5E3CD0) | 384 | no | [NOT byte-identical] placement-dependent (relative E8 calls to `KoreReallocValueStack` / `KoreConcatValues` / `KoreLookupMetamethod` / `KoreTablePanic` / `KoreInvokeValue` and the high-VA `off_1C830B8` type-name array via the panic). cdecl 6 args: `(vmcore, base, code, dest, startIdx, endIdx)` â€” the `".."` concat opcode (0x36) helper. Folds the value-stack slots `[startIdx .. endIdx]` into one value: each step takes the two slots at indices i-1 and i; when both are numbers (type 3) or strings (type 4) the pair is copied to the stack top and folded by `KoreConcatValues` (sub_5DC2B0), otherwise the `".."` metamethod (operator id 9) is looked up with `KoreLookupMetamethod` (preferring the non-plain-string side as the receiver when exactly one side is a plain string) and invoked with `KoreInvokeValue(vmcore, 2, 1, code)` â€” a nil metamethod pair panics `"operator %s is not supported for %s %s %s"` with the two `g_KoreTypeNames` type names; the folded result is popped back down to slot i-1. The value stack is grown up front when top + 24 would exceed capacity, the final value is copied from slot startIdx to the destination slot, and the (possibly relocated) value-stack base is returned. Replaces the no-op stub that wrote the nil pair into `*vmcore`. |

"Delete the (resolved) path of this shared slot": usercall (the slot address in EAX), resolve the slot through the path resolver (`ResolvePathSlot`, 0x18AFB90 -- the source slot is effectively ignored; the resolver stores a fresh block holding the process CWD (ANSI text, trailing backslash appended) in the local slot), `DeleteFileA` the resolved text (the empty string when the local block is null), then, on both branches, drop the local block's reference (`InterlockedDecrement`; free at zero) and decrement `g_Addend`, returning `DeleteFileA`'s 1/0 result. Because the resolver returns the CWD -- a directory -- `DeleteFileA` always fails and 0 is returned. Defined in gamemain.cpp next to `SharedFileExists` (its sibling -- same resolver + Str1-fallback + release shape); exposed in gamemain.h. Callers: `SaveSettingsBeforeExit` (0x18F71E0, worker.cpp -- the AccessibilitySettings.xml delete) and the worker `WM_CLOSE` path (DispatchWorkerMessage, the abnormal_shutdown.tmp marker). |

| `EntitySubObject` (0x6E2BB0) | 71 | no | [NOT byte-identical] placement-dependent (relative E8 calls to `__invalid_parameter_noinfo`) so C++ reimplementation. The entity sub-object build/validate (thiscall, plain retn): reads `*(this + 4)` as the sub-object pointer `v1`, derefs it (`v2 = *v1`), and on a null `v2` reports `F3InvalidParam` and returns 0 (the original is noreturn here); then reads `v3 = *(v1 + 0x14)` and `v4 = *(v3 + 4)`, loads `v5 = *v2`, checks `v4 == *(v5 + 0x14)` (report + continue), re-derefs `v2 = *v2`, checks `v4 == *(v2 + 0x14)` again (the re-deref yields the same value as `v5`, so this is the original's twin check), and returns `*(v4 + 0x8)`. The twin checks and the dead `v2 == 0` guard paths are preserved verbatim in behavior; the null-guard early return is the honest stand-in for the original's noreturn invalid-parameter report. Sole caller `PauseGameOnVMState` (sub_61A330, dead in this build). |

| `VfsQueryTableBuild` (0x1473E70) + `VfsQueryTableDrain` (0x1474320) | 192 / 127 | no | [NOT byte-identical] placement-dependent (relative E8 calls to the clock / VfsQueryTableDrain / CsGuardAcquire / CsGuardRelease / SetFrontendEvent / InterlockedExchangeAdd IAT thunks, the high-VA cache global `g_unk_1DDC830`, the control-block wrapper at o+4, and the indirect dispatch through the ring slot's vtable). `VfsQueryTableBuild` (thiscall, void, retn 4) takes the main allocation-cache object (`unk_1DDC830`) under the +0x34 critical section (an 8-byte `F3CsGuard` over the guard block) and, when the cache is initialised (+0x18 byte != 0), computes the pending-query count as the ring's used counter (the control block's +0x10C word) over the element size (+0x14) -- the count divides unguarded, so a zero element size traps, as the original does -- and then, for each pending query, reads the ring cursor (+0x108, ring base = the wrapper's v7[0]), dispatches the object at the cursor (ring + 0x110; `slot1(slot)` = vtable[1], then `slot0(slot, 0)` = vtable[0] with the zero second arg), drains one query via `VfsQueryTableDrain`, and breaks out once `clock() - start > timeout` (the unsigned compare; the timeout is the `a2` stack argument -- the descriptor's +0x04 worker count -- and the guard block is the original's 8-byte BYREF). `VfsQueryTableDrain` (thiscall, BOOL, retn 4) advances the ring cursor (+0x108, wrapping to 0 at the +0x100 limit and `int 3` on an overrun), decrements the used counter (+0x10C, InterlockedExchangeAdd by -elemSize), and returns the SetEvent result of the frontend event at wrapper +0x88 (the second EventObjCtor event wrapper; SetFrontendEvent does SetEvent on +0x80 of it). The InitVirtualFileSystem descriptor (+0x04/+0x08) is the worker count / ideal-processor pair; the build path's `VfsGetQueryTable` non-drain branch (g_dword_1DDC824 != 2) calls `VfsQueryTableBuild(g_unk_1DDC830, desc[1])`. `VfsGetQueryTable`'s drain-mode loop (g_dword_1DDC824 == 2) is also implemented, reading the used counter at the ring base +0x10C (the original's location) rather than at the wrapper +0x10C (which is one byte past the end of the 268-byte wrapper, and would have read a garbage location with the now-real Drain). Replaces the `sub_1473E70` / `sub_1474320` stubs in `gamemain.cpp`. |

| `F3AStringAssign` (0x402F00) | 214 (target) | no | [NOT byte-identical] placement-dependent (the LHAlloc / LHFree / _memcpy_s E8 calls, the length_error / out_of_range / bad_alloc throw helpers, and the high-VA heap table dword_2075B24). The semantics (faithful to the original): the self-overlap guard (Source points into [buffer, buffer+len)) -- the erase/replace copy sub_401BD0 does: the string becomes the segment [Source, Source+min(len-offset, SourceSize)) (memmove), then the growth path (sub_402C00 semantics) runs when the erased segment no longer fits; Source == null skips the overlap check (the len == 0 branch then applies: len = 0, buffer[0] = 0, capacity kept); SourceSize == 0xFFFFFFFF throws std::length_error("string too long") (the original's sub_15196EA); the zero-length path zeroes length + buffer[0] inline or heap (the original's zero branch); the SSO fast path (len <= capacity) copies in place and null-terminates; the heap growth path (len > capacity) mirrors sub_402C00 -- new capacity = len | 0xF (with the half-growth rule: when (newCap/3) < (oldCap>>1), new cap = oldCap + (oldCap>>1)), LHAlloc(cap+1), the old heap buffer freed, the stale old-content copy, the new content copied, length + terminator written, capacity updated. Replaces the `sub_402F00` no-op stub in `gamemain.cpp` (the modelHdr "globals_model_headers" path now calls it with a real F3AString); the SSO path is also reached by ReleaseLipCultures (sub_718140, the "lip%u" names). IDA renamed from `sub_402F00` to `F3AStringAssign`. |

| `FlushFrameStateBuffers` (0x18DDDB0) + `PoisonFirstFrameRegion` (0x590030) | 66 (target) | no | [NOT byte-identical] placement-dependent (relative E8 calls to the sibling `PoisonFirstFrameRegion`, the memset IAT slot, and the guarded vtable dispatches through the high-VA device global `dword_2074540`) and refs the high-VA state globals `unk_21153C0` / `qword_21163C0` / `dword_21163E0` (0x21153C0 / 0x21163C0 / 0x21163E0) and `unk_1DB13C0` / `qword_1DB21C0` / `dword_1DB21DC` (0x1DB13C0 / 0x1DB21C0 / 0x1DB21DC). Replaces the no-op `sub_18DDDB0` stub in `gamemain.cpp`. IDA names both functions already: `FlushFrameStateBuffers` (0x18DDDB0) and `PoisonFirstFrameRegion` (0x590030). |

| `F3InitAdapterState` (0x18F1150, 0x18F1150 - 0x18F11B3) + its helper chain (`F3QueryAdapterState` (0x1887790) / `F3IsOsMajorVersionAtLeast6` (0x1887820) / `F3QueryAdapterViaExtEscape` (0x1887880)) | 99 (target) | no | [NOT byte-identical] placement-dependent (relative calls to `F3QueryAdapterState` + the high-VA globals `dword_1E2EDBC` (0x01E2EDBC, the adapter index) and `dword_1D9E184` (0x01D9E184, the state word) + the `__security_cookie` prologue). To byte-match the placement-independent functions that reference IAT/data
globals, those globals must be placed at their original VAs (a PE
post-processor that adds high-RVA sections + reference patching, growing the
image toward the original's ~30 MB). Deferred until the module grows enough
that it is worth doing.

| `WriteStreamEnterElement` (0x17DAC00) + its node-building chain (`XmlChunkArrayEnsure` 0x17E4A10 / `XmlPoolAlloc` 0x17E4BA0 / `XmlNodeAlloc` 0x17E7110 / `XmlInsertNodeIntoSection` 0x17E6DE0 / `XmlBlockStoreNode` 0x17E65D0 (+ the XmlBlockTeardown chain 0x17E70D0 / XmlTeardownChildChain 0x17E7020 / XmlAttrChainTeardown 0x17E6F70 / XmlAttrChainFree 0x17E7350 / XmlChildChainFree 0x17E73D0 / XmlDecrementPoolCounter 0x17FD0F0) | 115 (target) | no | [NOT byte-identical] placement-dependent (relative calls to XmlHeapAlloc / InitXmlFileBlock / XmlPoolAlloc / XmlChunkArrayEnsure / InternXmlString / XmlInsertNodeIntoSection / XmlBlockStoreNode). The write-stream vtable[1] slot (enter the named element on the stream). Allocates the 8-byte {0, 4096-byte buffer} file block for the stream (InitXmlFileBlock, shared with the read side), allocates the 44-byte element node from the 4096-byte chunk pool (XmlPoolAlloc: the grow path installs a fresh chunk and one more chunk-array entry via XmlChunkArrayEnsure, the tail returns the remaining-space address and bumps the length; the node carries the interned name handle [2], the empty-string handle [3], and zeroed fields [0..1, 4..10]), and inserts it into the current section (XmlInsertNodeIntoSection: the child-chain splice behind the current child, this[7]++ and the node's [4] parent-link stamped) or stores it in the file block (XmlBlockStoreNode: the block[0] = node side effect plus the XmlBlockTeardown chain -- XmlTeardownChildChain (the child-chain recursive teardown: recurse the child, advance the head / fix the predecessor link, null the links and decrement the count, tear down the child's attribute chain, recurse the child's own child chain via XmlChildChainFree), XmlAttrChainTeardown (unlink + null the attribute chain + counters), XmlAttrChainFree (zero the chain slots) and XmlChildChainFree (recursive destructor: null the child's fields, recurse, zero the chain slots); the individual pool-node/attribute-node heap frees in the original are not reproduced (the nodes are pool-carved -- freeing them would be an invalid LHFree; they die with the pool chunks)). The node becomes the current section, the section-info slot is cleared, and 1 is returned. The write-side the bool writer (vt[5]) and the int2 writer (vt[8], now implemented -- the `%d` format + interning + section-info propagation, with the conditional vtable[11] dispatch skipped because this build installs only vt[1]/[3]/[5]/[8]/[9]) and the int writer (vt[9], `XmlWriteInt`, a thin forwarder to the vt[8] int value-writer) are the write-side slots installed; the write-side int writers still produce no stream output (the conditional vtable[11] dispatch is skipped), so no values are serialized and the file gets only the XML prologue; vt[3] (WriteXmlStreamAdvance) is wired for real. Replaces the placeholder enter-element stub in gamemain.cpp; the write-stream ctor (CXMLWriteStreamCtor) now wires vt[3] / vt[5] / vt[8] / vt[9] alongside vt[1]. IDA renamed the six teardown-chain helpers (sub_17E70D0 -> XmlBlockTeardown, sub_17E7020 -> XmlTeardownChildChain, sub_17E6F70 -> XmlAttrChainTeardown, sub_17E7350 -> XmlAttrChainFree, sub_17E73D0 -> XmlChildChainFree, sub_17FD0F0 -> XmlDecrementPoolCounter). |

## Original entry chain (for reference)

```
entry 0x1523B39:   call ___security_init_cookie; jmp ___tmainCRTStartup
0x15239BB:         __tmainCRTStartup  ->  call _WinMain@16
0x005CF170:        WinMain  (369 bytes)
```

`WinMain`: single-instance mutex `Local\FableIII` (focus the existing window
on a second launch) â†’ `FrameworkInit` â†’ parse `-loadsavegame <path>` â†’
`RunGame` (run the game) â†’ `FrameworkShutdown` â†’ return the exit code.
